```python
 107 | 
 108 | @dataclass
 109 | class FileAnalysis:
 110 |     """Single-file analysis result from libclang."""
 111 |     preamble: str
 112 |     functions: list[FunctionDef] = field(default_factory=list)
 113 |     classes: list[ClassDef] = field(default_factory=list)
 114 | 
 115 |     def build_local_index(self) -> ProjectIndex:
 116 |         """Build a ProjectIndex from this file's functions for dep computation."""
 117 |         idx = ProjectIndex()
 118 |         for func in self.functions:
 119 |             idx.add_function(func)
 120 |         idx.compute_dep_levels()
 121 |         return idx
 122 | 
 123 | 
 124 | @dataclass
 125 | class HunkRange:
 126 |     """Parsed unified diff hunk range."""
 127 |     old_start: int
 128 |     old_count: int
 129 |     new_start: int
 130 |     new_count: int
 131 | 
 132 | 
 133 | # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
 134 | # Diff parsing
 135 | # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
 136 | 
 137 | _HUNK_RE = re.compile(r'@@ -(\d+)(?:,(\d+))? \+(\d+)(?:,(\d+))? @@')
 138 | 
 139 | 
 140 | def parse_hunk_ranges(diff: str) -> list[HunkRange]:
 141 |     """Parse unified diff @@ lines into hunk ranges."""
 142 |     return [
 143 |         HunkRange(
 144 |             old_start=int(m.group(1)),
 145 |             old_count=int(m.group(2) or '1'),
 146 |             new_start=int(m.group(3)),
 147 |             new_count=int(m.group(4) or '1'),
 148 |         )
 149 |         for m in _HUNK_RE.finditer(diff)
 150 |     ]
 151 | 
 152 | 
 153 | def changed_lines(hunks: list[HunkRange], use_old: bool) -> set[int]:
 154 |     """Get set of line numbers touched by hunks (1-based)."""
 155 |     lines: set[int] = set()
 156 |     for h in hunks:
 157 |         start = h.old_start if use_old else h.new_start
 158 |         count = max(h.old_count if use_old else h.new_count, 1)
 159 |         lines.update(range(start, start + count))
 160 |     return lines
 161 | 
 162 | 
 163 | # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
 164 | # Clang single-file analysis
 165 | # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
 166 | 
 167 | _C_EXTENSIONS = {'.c'}
 168 | _SIMPLE_FALLBACK_ARGS_CPP = ['-fsyntax-only', '-Wno-everything', '-std=c++17']
 169 | _SIMPLE_FALLBACK_ARGS_C = ['-x', 'c', '-fsyntax-only', '-Wno-everything', '-std=c11']
 170 | 
 171 | 
 172 | def analyze_file_clang(
 173 |     content: str,
 174 |     filepath: str,
 175 |     clang_index: Index,
 176 |     tmpdir: str,
 177 | ) -> FileAnalysis:
 178 |     """Parse a single file's content with libclang and extract functions/classes.
 179 | 
 180 |     Writes content to a temp file (clang needs files on disk), parses with
 181 |     PARSE_INCOMPLETE to tolerate errors, then extracts function definitions
 182 |     with qualified names and call references.
 183 |     """
 184 |     if not content or len(content) < 20:
 185 |         return FileAnalysis(preamble='')
 186 | 
 187 |     ext = Path(filepath).suffix.lower() or '.cpp'
 188 |     tmp_path = os.path.join(tmpdir, f"source{ext}")
 189 | 
 190 |     with open(tmp_path, 'w', errors='replace') as f:
 191 |         f.write(content)
 192 | 
 193 |     if ext in _C_EXTENSIONS:
 194 |         compile_args = list(_SIMPLE_FALLBACK_ARGS_C)
 195 |     else:
 196 |         compile_args = list(_SIMPLE_FALLBACK_ARGS_CPP)
 197 | 
 198 |     try:
 199 |         tu = clang_index.parse(
 200 |             tmp_path,
 201 |             args=compile_args,
 202 |             options=(
 203 |                 TranslationUnit.PARSE_INCOMPLETE
 204 |                 | TranslationUnit.PARSE_PRECOMPILED_PREAMBLE
 205 |             ),
 206 |         )
 207 |     except Exception:
 208 |         return FileAnalysis(preamble='')
 209 | 
 210 |     # Extract preamble
 211 |     preamble = extract_preamble(tu, tmp_path)
 212 | 
 213 |     # Extract functions and classes
 214 |     functions: list[FunctionDef] = []
 215 |     classes: list[ClassDef] = []
 216 | 
 217 |     def visit(cursor):
 218 |         if not cursor.location.file:
 219 |             return
 220 |         if cursor.location.file.name != tmp_path:
 221 |             return
 222 | 
 223 |         if cursor.kind in FUNCTION_KINDS and cursor.is_definition():
 224 |             text = get_function_text(cursor, tu)
 225 |             if text and len(text) >= 20:
 226 |                 callees = extract_callees(cursor)
 227 |                 qname = get_qualified_name(cursor)
 228 |                 start_line = cursor.extent.start.line
 229 |                 end_line = cursor.extent.end.line
 230 |                 functions.append(FunctionDef(
 231 |                     name=cursor.spelling,
 232 |                     qualified_name=qname,
 233 |                     file=filepath,
 234 |                     line=start_line,
 235 |                     text=text,
 236 |                     callees=callees,
 237 |                     is_definition=True,
 238 |                     end_line=end_line,
 239 |                 ))
 240 | 
 241 |         elif cursor.kind in (CursorKind.CLASS_DECL, CursorKind.STRUCT_DECL,
 242 |                               CursorKind.CLASS_TEMPLATE):
 243 |             if cursor.is_definition():
 244 |                 text = get_function_text(cursor, tu)
 245 |                 if text and len(text) >= 20:
 246 |                     qname = get_qualified_name(cursor)
 247 |                     classes.append(ClassDef(
 248 |                         name=cursor.spelling,
 249 |                         qualified_name=qname,
 250 |                         text=text,
 251 |                         start_line=cursor.extent.start.line,
 252 |                         end_line=cursor.extent.end.line,
 253 |                     ))
 254 |                 # Recurse into class children to extract inline methods,
 255 |                 # constructors, and destructors defined inside the class body.
 256 |                 for child in cursor.get_children():
 257 |                     visit(child)
 258 | 
 259 |         elif cursor.kind in CONTAINER_KINDS:
 260 |             for child in cursor.get_children():
 261 |                 visit(child)
 262 | 
 263 |     for cursor in tu.cursor.get_children():
 264 |         visit(cursor)
 265 | 
 266 |     return FileAnalysis(preamble=preamble, functions=functions, classes=classes)
 267 | 
 268 | 
 269 | # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
 270 | # Changed function/class detection
 271 | # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
 272 | 
 273 | def find_changed_functions(
 274 |     analysis: FileAnalysis,
 275 |     changed_line_set: set[int],
 276 | ) -> list[int]:
 277 |     """Return indices of functions whose line spans overlap changed lines."""
 278 |     result = []
 279 |     for i, func in enumerate(analysis.functions):
 280 |         end_line = func.end_line
 281 |         func_lines = set(range(func.line, end_line + 1))
 282 |         if func_lines & changed_line_set:
 283 |             result.append(i)
 284 |     return result
 285 | 
 286 | 
 287 | def find_changed_classes(
 288 |     analysis: FileAnalysis,
 289 |     changed_line_set: set[int],
 290 | ) -> list[int]:
 291 |     """Return indices of classes whose line spans overlap changed lines."""
 292 |     result = []
 293 |     for i, cls in enumerate(analysis.classes):
 294 |         cls_lines = set(range(cls.start_line, cls.end_line + 1))
 295 |         if cls_lines & changed_line_set:
 296 |             result.append(i)
 297 |     return result
 298 | 
 299 | 
 300 | # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
 301 | # Symbol-level change tracking
 302 | # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
 303 | 
 304 | @dataclass
 305 | class SymbolEntry:
 306 |     """A symbol (function or class) with a stable integer ID.
 307 | 
 308 |     ID scheme: functions are numbered 0..N-1, classes are numbered N..N+M-1
 309 |     where N = len(analysis.functions), M = len(analysis.classes).
 310 |     """
 311 |     id: int
 312 |     name: str
 313 |     qualified_name: str
 314 |     kind: str  # 'function' or 'class'
 315 |     start_line: int
 316 |     end_line: int
 317 | 
 318 | 
 319 | def build_symbol_table(analysis: FileAnalysis) -> list[SymbolEntry]:
 320 |     """Build a flat symbol table from functions and classes.
 321 | 
 322 |     Functions get IDs 0..N-1, classes get IDs N..N+M-1.
 323 |     """
 324 |     symbols: list[SymbolEntry] = []
 325 |     for i, func in enumerate(analysis.functions):
 326 |         symbols.append(SymbolEntry(
 327 |             id=i,
 328 |             name=func.name,
 329 |             qualified_name=func.qualified_name,
 330 |             kind='function',
 331 |             start_line=func.line,
 332 |             end_line=func.end_line,
 333 |         ))
 334 |     n_funcs = len(analysis.functions)
 335 |     for j, cls in enumerate(analysis.classes):
 336 |         symbols.append(SymbolEntry(
 337 |             id=n_funcs + j,
 338 |             name=cls.name,
 339 |             qualified_name=cls.qualified_name,
 340 |             kind='class',
 341 |             start_line=cls.start_line,
 342 |             end_line=cls.end_line,
 343 |         ))
 344 |     return symbols
 345 | 
 346 | 
 347 | def compute_changed_symbol_ids(
 348 |     analysis: FileAnalysis,
 349 |     changed_line_set: set[int],
 350 | ) -> list[int]:
 351 |     """Return stable IDs of symbols whose line spans overlap changed lines.
 352 | 
 353 |     Uses the same ID scheme as build_symbol_table(): functions first, then
 354 |     classes.
 355 |     """
 356 |     ids: list[int] = []
 357 |     for i, func in enumerate(analysis.functions):
 358 |         func_lines = set(range(func.line, func.end_line + 1))
 359 |         if func_lines & changed_line_set:
 360 |             ids.append(i)
 361 |     n_funcs = len(analysis.functions)
 362 |     for j, cls in enumerate(analysis.classes):
 363 |         cls_lines = set(range(cls.start_line, cls.end_line + 1))
 364 |         if cls_lines & changed_line_set:
 365 |             ids.append(n_funcs + j)
 366 |     return ids
 367 | 
 368 | 
 369 | def compute_ripple_candidates(
 370 |     analysis: FileAnalysis,
 371 |     changed_symbol_ids: list[int],
 372 |     max_depth: int = 3,
 373 | ) -> list[dict]:
 374 |     """For each changed symbol, find callers and type dependents that might
 375 |     need updating.
 376 | 
 377 |     Returns a list of dicts:
 378 |         {
 379 |             'changed_symbol_id': int,
 380 |             'changed_symbol_name': str,
 381 |             'candidates': [
 382 |                 {
 383 |                     'symbol_id': int,
 384 |                     'symbol_name': str,
 385 |                     'relation': str,  # 'caller' | 'type_dependent' | 'co_member'
 386 |                     'depth': int,     # BFS depth from the changed symbol
 387 |                 }
 388 |             ]
 389 |         }
 390 | 
 391 |     Ripple relations:
 392 |     - 'caller': a function that calls the changed function (reverse edge)
 393 |     - 'type_dependent': a function that uses a changed class as parameter/return type
 394 |     - 'co_member': another method in the same class as a changed method
 395 |     """
 396 |     if not changed_symbol_ids:
 397 |         return []
 398 | 
 399 |     symbols = build_symbol_table(analysis)
 400 |     id_to_sym = {s.id: s for s in symbols}
 401 |     n_funcs = len(analysis.functions)
 402 | 
 403 |     # Build reverse call graph: callee_qname -> list of caller symbol IDs
 404 |     callee_to_callers: dict[str, list[int]] = defaultdict(list)
 405 |     for i, func in enumerate(analysis.functions):
 406 |         for callee_qname in func.callees:
 407 |             callee_to_callers[callee_qname].append(i)
 408 | 
 409 |     # Build class membership: class_qname -> list of function symbol IDs
 410 |     # (functions whose qualified_name starts with "ClassName::")
 411 |     class_members: dict[str, list[int]] = defaultdict(list)
 412 |     for j, cls in enumerate(analysis.classes):
 413 |         prefix = cls.qualified_name + '::'
 414 |         for i, func in enumerate(analysis.functions):
 415 |             if func.qualified_name.startswith(prefix):
 416 |                 class_members[cls.qualified_name].append(i)
 417 | 
 418 |     # Build type usage: for each function, which classes appear in its line range
 419 |     # (heuristic: class name appears in function text)
 420 |     func_uses_class: dict[int, list[int]] = defaultdict(list)
 421 |     for i, func in enumerate(analysis.functions):
 422 |         for j, cls in enumerate(analysis.classes):
 423 |             if cls.name in func.text:
 424 |                 func_uses_class[i].append(n_funcs + j)
 425 | 
 426 |     result = []
 427 |     for changed_id in changed_symbol_ids:
 428 |         sym = id_to_sym.get(changed_id)
 429 |         if sym is None:
 430 |             continue
 431 | 
 432 |         candidates: list[dict] = []
 433 |         seen: set[int] = {changed_id}
 434 | 
 435 |         if sym.kind == 'function':
 436 |             # BFS through reverse call graph to find callers
 437 |             queue = deque([(sym.qualified_name, 1)])
 438 |             visited_qnames: set[str] = {sym.qualified_name}
 439 |             while queue:
 440 |                 qname, depth = queue.popleft()
 441 |                 if depth > max_depth:
 442 |                     continue
 443 |                 for caller_id in callee_to_callers.get(qname, []):
 444 |                     if caller_id not in seen:
 445 |                         seen.add(caller_id)
 446 |                         caller_func = analysis.functions[caller_id]
 447 |                         candidates.append({
 448 |                             'symbol_id': caller_id,
 449 |                             'symbol_name': caller_func.qualified_name,
 450 |                             'relation': 'caller',
 451 |                             'depth': depth,
 452 |                         })
 453 |                         if depth < max_depth and caller_func.qualified_name not in visited_qnames:
 454 |                             visited_qnames.add(caller_func.qualified_name)
 455 |                             queue.append((caller_func.qualified_name, depth + 1))
 456 | 
 457 |             # co_member: other methods in the same class
 458 |             for cls_qname, member_ids in class_members.items():
 459 |                 if changed_id in member_ids:
 460 |                     for mid in member_ids:
 461 |                         if mid not in seen:
 462 |                             seen.add(mid)
 463 |                             candidates.append({
 464 |                                 'symbol_id': mid,
 465 |                                 'symbol_name': analysis.functions[mid].qualified_name,
 466 |                                 'relation': 'co_member',
 467 |                                 'depth': 1,
 468 |                             })
 469 | 
 470 |         elif sym.kind == 'class':
 471 |             cls_idx = changed_id - n_funcs
 472 |             cls = analysis.classes[cls_idx]
 473 |             # type_dependent: functions that mention this class
 474 |             for i, func in enumerate(analysis.functions):
 475 |                 if i not in seen and cls.name in func.text:
 476 |                     seen.add(i)
 477 |                     candidates.append({
 478 |                         'symbol_id': i,
 479 |                         'symbol_name': func.qualified_name,
 480 |                         'relation': 'type_dependent',
 481 |                         'depth': 1,
 482 |                     })
 483 |             # co_member: methods of this class
 484 |             for mid in class_members.get(cls.qualified_name, []):
 485 |                 if mid not in seen:
 486 |                     seen.add(mid)
 487 |                     candidates.append({
 488 |                         'symbol_id': mid,
 489 |                         'symbol_name': analysis.functions[mid].qualified_name,
 490 |                         'relation': 'co_member',
 491 |                         'depth': 1,
 492 |                     })
 493 | 
 494 |         if candidates:
 495 |             result.append({
 496 |                 'changed_symbol_id': changed_id,
 497 |                 'changed_symbol_name': sym.qualified_name,
 498 |                 'candidates': candidates,
 499 |             })
 500 | 
 501 |     return result
 502 | 
 503 | 
 504 | # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
 505 | # Transitive dependency chain extraction
 506 | # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
 507 | 
 508 | def extract_function_chain(
 509 |     analysis: FileAnalysis,
 510 |     changed_indices: list[int],
 511 |     max_depth: int = 5,
 512 | ) -> list[FunctionDef]:
 513 |     """Extract changed functions + transitive deps, sorted by dep_level (leaves first)."""
 514 |     if not changed_indices:
 515 |         return []
 516 | 
 517 |     # Build local index for dep computation
 518 |     idx = analysis.build_local_index()
 519 | 
 520 |     # Collect all transitive deps for each changed function
 521 |     all_qnames = set()
 522 |     for i in changed_indices:
 523 |         func = analysis.functions[i]
 524 |         all_qnames.add(func.qualified_name)
 525 |         # BFS through local call graph
 526 |         visited = {func.qualified_name}
 527 |         queue = deque([(func.qualified_name, 0)])
 528 |         while queue:
 529 |             qname, depth = queue.popleft()
 530 |             if depth >= max_depth:
 531 |                 continue
 532 |             f = idx.functions.get(qname)
 533 |             if not f:
 534 |                 continue
 535 |             for callee in f.callees:
 536 |                 if callee not in visited and callee in idx.functions:
 537 |                     visited.add(callee)
 538 |                     all_qnames.add(callee)
 539 |                     queue.append((callee, depth + 1))
 540 | 
 541 |     # Collect FunctionDefs, sorted by dep_level (leaves first)
 542 |     result = []
 543 |     for func in analysis.functions:
 544 |         if func.qualified_name in all_qnames:
 545 |             result.append(func)
 546 |     result.sort(key=lambda f: f.dep_level)
 547 |     return result
 548 | 
 549 | 
 550 | # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
 551 | # Document formatting
 552 | # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
 553 | 
 554 | def build_docstring(record: dict) -> str:
 555 |     """Build C++ docstring comment from commit metadata."""
 556 |     parts = ['/**']
 557 |     subject = record.get('subject', '').strip()
 558 |     if subject:
 559 |         parts.append(f' * @brief {subject}')
 560 |         parts.append(' *')
 561 | 
 562 |     repo = record.get('repo', '')
 563 |     filepath = record.get('filepath', '')
 564 |     if repo:
 565 |         parts.append(f' * Repository: {repo}')
 566 |     if filepath:
 567 |         parts.append(f' * File: {filepath}')
 568 | 
 569 |     body = record.get('body', '').strip()
 570 |     if body:
 571 |         parts.append(' *')
 572 |         for line in body.splitlines()[:8]:
 573 |             parts.append(f' * {line.rstrip()}')
 574 | 
 575 |     parts.append(' */')
 576 |     return '\n'.join(parts)
 577 | 
 578 | 
 579 | def format_chain_document(
 580 |     record: dict,
 581 |     old_analysis: FileAnalysis,
 582 |     new_analysis: FileAnalysis,
 583 |     hunks: list[HunkRange],
 584 |     max_dep_depth: int = 5,
 585 | ) -> Optional[dict]:
 586 |     """Format A: PRE-COMMIT chain → POST-COMMIT chain with enriched metadata."""
 587 |     old_lines = changed_lines(hunks, use_old=True)
 588 |     new_lines = changed_lines(hunks, use_old=False)
 589 | 
 590 |     old_changed_funcs = find_changed_functions(old_analysis, old_lines)
 591 |     new_changed_funcs = find_changed_functions(new_analysis, new_lines)
 592 |     old_changed_classes = find_changed_classes(old_analysis, old_lines)
 593 |     new_changed_classes = find_changed_classes(new_analysis, new_lines)
 594 | 
 595 |     if (not old_changed_funcs and not new_changed_funcs
 596 |             and not old_changed_classes and not new_changed_classes):
 597 |         return None
 598 | 
 599 |     old_chain = extract_function_chain(old_analysis, old_changed_funcs, max_dep_depth)
 600 |     new_chain = extract_function_chain(new_analysis, new_changed_funcs, max_dep_depth)
 601 | 
 602 |     # Build parts_info: (text, kind, dep_level, name, qname)
 603 |     parts_info: list[PartInfo] = []
 604 | 
 605 |     # Docstring (kind=6: comment)
 606 |     docstring = build_docstring(record)
 607 |     parts_info.append((docstring, 6, 0, '', None))
 608 | 
 609 |     # PRE-COMMIT section
 610 |     if old_chain or old_changed_classes:
 611 |         parts_info.append(('// === PRE-COMMIT ===', 0, 0, '', None))
 612 |         short_preamble = '\n'.join(old_analysis.preamble.splitlines()[:20])
 613 |         if short_preamble:
 614 |             parts_info.append((short_preamble, 1, 0, '', None))
 615 |         for ci in old_changed_classes:
 616 |             cls = old_analysis.classes[ci]
 617 |             parts_info.append((cls.text, 4, 0, cls.name, cls.qualified_name))
 618 |         for func in old_chain:
 619 |             parts_info.append((func.text, 3, func.dep_level, func.name, func.qualified_name))
 620 | 
 621 |     # POST-COMMIT section
 622 |     subject = record.get('subject', '').strip()
 623 |     if new_chain or new_changed_classes:
 624 |         parts_info.append((f'// === POST-COMMIT: {subject} ===', 0, 0, '', None))
 625 |         short_preamble = '\n'.join(new_analysis.preamble.splitlines()[:20])
 626 |         if short_preamble:
 627 |             parts_info.append((short_preamble, 1, 0, '', None))
 628 |         for ci in new_changed_classes:
 629 |             cls = new_analysis.classes[ci]
 630 |             parts_info.append((cls.text, 4, 0, cls.name, cls.qualified_name))
 631 |         for func in new_chain:
 632 |             parts_info.append((func.text, 3, func.dep_level, func.name, func.qualified_name))
 633 | 
 634 |     if len(parts_info) <= 1:
 635 |         return None
 636 | 
 637 |     # Compute changed_symbol_ids and ripple_candidates from the NEW analysis
 638 |     # (post-commit state is what the model learns to predict).  Fall back to
 639 |     # old analysis when new is empty.
 640 |     primary = new_analysis if new_analysis.functions or new_analysis.classes else old_analysis
 641 |     csids = compute_changed_symbol_ids(primary, new_lines if (new_analysis.functions or new_analysis.classes) else old_lines)
 642 |     ripple = compute_ripple_candidates(primary, csids)
 643 | 
 644 |     return _build_enriched_from_parts(
 645 |         parts_info, old_analysis, new_analysis, record,
 646 |         changed_symbol_ids=csids, ripple_candidates=ripple,
 647 |     )
 648 | 
 649 | 
 650 | def format_diff_document(
 651 |     record: dict,
 652 |     old_analysis: FileAnalysis,
 653 |     hunks: list[HunkRange],
 654 |     max_dep_depth: int = 5,
 655 | ) -> Optional[dict]:
 656 |     """Format B: Context code from old version + raw unified diff."""
 657 |     old_lines = changed_lines(hunks, use_old=True)
 658 |     old_changed_funcs = find_changed_functions(old_analysis, old_lines)
 659 |     old_changed_classes = find_changed_classes(old_analysis, old_lines)
 660 | 
 661 |     if not old_changed_funcs and not old_changed_classes:
 662 |         return None
 663 | 
 664 |     old_chain = extract_function_chain(old_analysis, old_changed_funcs, max_dep_depth)
 665 | 
 666 |     parts_info: list[PartInfo] = []
 667 | 
 668 |     # Docstring
 669 |     docstring = build_docstring(record)
 670 |     parts_info.append((docstring, 6, 0, '', None))
 671 | 
 672 |     # Context: old code chain
 673 |     parts_info.append(('// === CONTEXT ===', 0, 0, '', None))
 674 |     short_preamble = '\n'.join(old_analysis.preamble.splitlines()[:20])
 675 |     if short_preamble:
 676 |         parts_info.append((short_preamble, 1, 0, '', None))
 677 |     for ci in old_changed_classes:
 678 |         cls = old_analysis.classes[ci]
 679 |         parts_info.append((cls.text, 4, 0, cls.name, cls.qualified_name))
 680 |     for func in old_chain:
 681 |         parts_info.append((func.text, 3, func.dep_level, func.name, func.qualified_name))
 682 | 
 683 |     # Raw diff
 684 |     diff_text = record.get('diff', '')
 685 |     if diff_text:
 686 |         parts_info.append(('// === DIFF ===', 0, 0, '', None))
 687 |         parts_info.append((diff_text, 0, 0, '', None))
 688 | 
 689 |     if len(parts_info) <= 2:
 690 |         return None
 691 | 
 692 |     # Compute changed_symbol_ids and ripple_candidates from old analysis
 693 |     csids = compute_changed_symbol_ids(old_analysis, old_lines)
 694 |     ripple = compute_ripple_candidates(old_analysis, csids)
 695 | 
 696 |     return _build_enriched_from_parts(
 697 |         parts_info, old_analysis, None, record,
 698 |         changed_symbol_ids=csids, ripple_candidates=ripple,
 699 |     )
 700 | 
 701 | 
 702 | def _build_enriched_from_parts(
 703 |     parts_info: list[PartInfo],
 704 |     old_analysis: FileAnalysis,
 705 |     new_analysis: Optional[FileAnalysis],
 706 |     record: dict,
 707 |     changed_symbol_ids: Optional[list[int]] = None,
 708 |     ripple_candidates: Optional[list[dict]] = None,
 709 | ) -> dict:
 710 |     """Build enriched document dict from parts_info list.
 711 | 
 712 |     Produces the same schema as build_enriched_doc() in index_project.py:
 713 |     text, structure_ids, chunk_boundaries, call_edges, type_edges,
 714 |     ast_depth, sibling_index, ast_node_type.
 715 | 
 716 |     Additionally emits:
 717 |     - changed_symbol_ids: list of int — IDs of symbols modified in this commit
 718 |     - ripple_candidates: list of dicts — symbols that may need updating due to changes
 719 |     """
 720 |     texts = [p[0] for p in parts_info]
 721 |     full_text = '\n\n'.join(texts)
 722 |     text_len = len(full_text)
 723 | 
 724 |     structure_ids = [0] * text_len
 725 |     chunk_boundaries = []
 726 |     offset = 0
 727 | 
 728 |     # Maps for edge computation: chunk_idx -> (qname, callees_list)
 729 |     chunk_qnames: dict[int, str] = {}
 730 |     chunk_callees: dict[int, list[str]] = {}
 731 | 
 732 |     # Merge functions from both analyses for callee lookup
 733 |     all_funcs: dict[str, FunctionDef] = {}
 734 |     for func in old_analysis.functions:
 735 |         all_funcs[func.qualified_name] = func
 736 |     if new_analysis:
 737 |         for func in new_analysis.functions:
 738 |             all_funcs[func.qualified_name] = func
 739 | 
 740 |     for i, (part_text, kind, dep_level, name, qname) in enumerate(parts_info):
 741 |         part_len = len(part_text)
 742 |         if offset + part_len > text_len:
 743 |             break
 744 | 
 745 |         for j in range(offset, offset + part_len):
 746 |             structure_ids[j] = kind
 747 | 
 748 |         chunk_boundaries.append({
 749 |             'start': offset,
 750 |             'end': offset + part_len,
 751 |             'kind': kind,
 752 |             'dep_level': dep_level,
 753 |             'name': name,
 754 |         })
 755 | 
 756 |         if qname and qname in all_funcs:
 757 |             chunk_qnames[i] = qname
 758 |             chunk_callees[i] = all_funcs[qname].callees
 759 | 
 760 |         offset += part_len
 761 |         if i < len(parts_info) - 1:
 762 |             offset += 2  # "\n\n"
 763 | 
 764 |     # Compute call_edges between chunks
 765 |     call_edges = []
 766 |     for ci, caller_qname in chunk_qnames.items():
 767 |         callees = chunk_callees.get(ci, [])
 768 |         for callee_qname in callees:
 769 |             for cj, target_qname in chunk_qnames.items():
 770 |                 if ci != cj and target_qname == callee_qname:
 771 |                     call_edges.append({'from': ci, 'to': cj})
 772 | 
 773 |     type_edges: list[dict[str, object]] = []
 774 | 
 775 |     # AST metadata via tree-sitter
 776 |     ast_depth, sibling_index, ast_node_type = extract_ast_metadata(full_text)
 777 | 
 778 |     result: dict = {
 779 |         'text': full_text,
 780 |         'structure_ids': structure_ids,
 781 |         'chunk_boundaries': chunk_boundaries,
 782 |         'call_edges': call_edges,
 783 |         'type_edges': type_edges,
 784 |         'ast_depth': ast_depth,
 785 |         'sibling_index': sibling_index,
 786 |         'ast_node_type': ast_node_type,
 787 |         'changed_symbol_ids': changed_symbol_ids or [],
 788 |         'ripple_candidates': ripple_candidates or [],
 789 |     }
 790 | 
 791 |     # Language info detection
 792 |     filepath = record.get('filepath', '')
 793 |     if detect_language_info is not None:
 794 |         lang_info = detect_language_info(full_text, filepath)
 795 |         if lang_info:
 796 |             result['language_info'] = lang_info
 797 | 
 798 |     return result
 799 | 
 800 | 
 801 | # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
 802 | # Token counting
 803 | # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
 804 | 
 805 | _tokenizer = None
 806 | 
 807 | 
 808 | def _load_tokenizer(path: Optional[str]):
 809 |     global _tokenizer
 810 |     if _tokenizer is not None:
 811 |         return
 812 |     if path and os.path.exists(path):
 813 |         try:
 814 |             from tokenizers import Tokenizer
 815 |             _tokenizer = Tokenizer.from_file(path)
 816 |             return
 817 |         except Exception:
 818 |             pass
 819 |     # Fallback: estimate ~4 bytes per token
 820 |     _tokenizer = None
 821 | 
 822 | 
 823 | def count_tokens(text: str) -> int:
 824 |     if _tokenizer is not None:
 825 |         return len(_tokenizer.encode(text).ids)
 826 |     return max(1, len(text) // 4)
 827 | 
 828 | 
 829 | # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
 830 | # Main processing
 831 | # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
 832 | 
 833 | def process_record(
 834 |     record: dict,
 835 |     clang_index: Index,
 836 |     tmpdir: str,
 837 |     max_tokens: int,
 838 |     max_file_bytes: int,
 839 |     doc_format: str,
 840 |     max_dep_depth: int,
 841 | ) -> list[dict]:
 842 |     """Process a single commit record into enriched documents."""
 843 |     old_content = record.get('old_content', '')
 844 |     new_content = record.get('new_content', '')
 845 |     diff = record.get('diff', '')
 846 | 
 847 |     if len(old_content) > max_file_bytes or len(new_content) > max_file_bytes:
 848 |         return []
 849 |     if len(old_content) < 50 or len(new_content) < 50:
 850 |         return []
 851 | 
 852 |     hunks = parse_hunk_ranges(diff)
 853 |     if not hunks:
 854 |         return []
 855 | 
 856 |     filepath = record.get('filepath', 'source.cpp')
 857 | 
 858 |     # Parse old and new with clang (use separate temp subdirs to avoid conflicts)
 859 |     old_dir = os.path.join(tmpdir, 'old')
 860 |     new_dir = os.path.join(tmpdir, 'new')
 861 |     os.makedirs(old_dir, exist_ok=True)
 862 |     os.makedirs(new_dir, exist_ok=True)
 863 | 
 864 |     old_analysis = analyze_file_clang(old_content, filepath, clang_index, old_dir)
 865 |     new_analysis = analyze_file_clang(new_content, filepath, clang_index, new_dir)
 866 | 
 867 |     documents: list[dict[str, object]] = []
 868 | 
 869 |     if doc_format in ('chain', 'both'):
 870 |         doc = format_chain_document(record, old_analysis, new_analysis, hunks, max_dep_depth)
 871 |         if doc:
 872 |             tokens = count_tokens(doc['text'])
 873 |             if tokens <= max_tokens and len(doc['text']) >= 100:
 874 |                 doc['actual_token_count'] = tokens
 875 |                 documents.append(doc)
 876 | 
 877 |     if doc_format in ('diff', 'both'):
 878 |         doc = format_diff_document(record, old_analysis, hunks, max_dep_depth)
 879 |         if doc:
 880 |             tokens = count_tokens(doc['text'])
 881 |             if tokens <= max_tokens and len(doc['text']) >= 100:
 882 |                 doc['actual_token_count'] = tokens
 883 |                 documents.append(doc)
 884 | 
 885 |     return documents
 886 | 
 887 | 
 888 | def process_jsonl_file(
 889 |     input_path: str,
 890 |     output_file,
 891 |     clang_index: Index,
 892 |     tmpdir: str,
 893 |     max_tokens: int,
 894 |     max_file_bytes: int,
 895 |     doc_format: str,
 896 |     max_dep_depth: int,
 897 | ) -> dict:
 898 |     """Process a JSONL input file, writing enriched docs to output."""
 899 |     stats = {
 900 |         'records_read': 0,
 901 |         'documents_written': 0,
 902 |         'records_skipped': 0,
 903 |         'records_empty': 0,
 904 |         'parse_errors': 0,
 905 |     }
 906 |     seen_hashes: set[str] = set()
 907 | 
 908 |     with open(input_path, 'r', errors='replace') as f:
 909 |         for line_num, line in enumerate(f, 1):
 910 |             line = line.strip()
 911 |             if not line:
 912 |                 continue
 913 | 
 914 |             try:
 915 |                 record = json.loads(line)
 916 |             except json.JSONDecodeError:
 917 |                 stats['parse_errors'] += 1
 918 |                 continue
 919 | 
 920 |             stats['records_read'] += 1
 921 | 
 922 |             try:
 923 |                 docs = process_record(
 924 |                     record, clang_index, tmpdir,
 925 |                     max_tokens, max_file_bytes, doc_format, max_dep_depth,
 926 |                 )
 927 |             except Exception as e:
 928 |                 stats['parse_errors'] += 1
 929 |                 if stats['parse_errors'] <= 10:
 930 |                     print(f"  WARN: Record {line_num}: {e}", file=sys.stderr)
 931 |                 continue
 932 | 
 933 |             if not docs:
 934 |                 stats['records_empty'] += 1
 935 |                 continue
 936 | 
 937 |             for doc in docs:
 938 |                 doc_hash = hashlib.md5(doc['text'].encode()).hexdigest()
 939 |                 if doc_hash in seen_hashes:
 940 |                     stats['records_skipped'] += 1
 941 |                     continue
 942 |                 seen_hashes.add(doc_hash)
 943 |                 output_file.write(json.dumps(doc, ensure_ascii=False) + '\n')
 944 |                 stats['documents_written'] += 1
 945 | 
 946 |             if stats['records_read'] % 1000 == 0:
 947 |                 print(
 948 |                     f"  [{input_path}] {stats['records_read']} records, "
 949 |                     f"{stats['documents_written']} docs written",
 950 |                     file=sys.stderr,
 951 |                 )
 952 | 
 953 |     return stats
 954 | 
 955 | 
 956 | def main():
 957 |     parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(
 958 |         description='Process git commit diffs into enriched training documents using libclang',
 959 |     )
 960 |     parser.add_argument(
 961 |         '--inputs', required=True, nargs='+',
 962 |         help='Input JSONL files with commit records (old_content, new_content, diff, ...)',
 963 |     )
 964 |     parser.add_argument(
 965 |         '--output', required=True,
 966 |         help='Output enriched JSONL file',
 967 |     )
 968 |     parser.add_argument(
 969 |         '--max-tokens', type=int, default=4096,
 970 |         help='Maximum tokens per document (default: 4096)',
 971 |     )
 972 |     parser.add_argument(
 973 |         '--max-file-bytes', type=int, default=500000,
 974 |         help='Skip files larger than this (default: 500000)',
 975 |     )
 976 |     parser.add_argument(
 977 |         '--max-dep-depth', type=int, default=5,
 978 |         help='Maximum BFS depth for transitive deps (default: 5)',
 979 |     )
 980 |     parser.add_argument(
 981 |         '--format', choices=['chain', 'diff', 'both'], default='both',
 982 |         dest='doc_format',
 983 |         help='Document format: chain (PRE/POST), diff (context+patch), or both',
 984 |     )
 985 |     parser.add_argument(
 986 |         '--tokenizer-path', default=None,
 987 |         help='Path to tokenizer.json for exact token counting (fallback: estimate)',
 988 |     )
 989 |     parser.add_argument(
 990 |         '--libclang-path', default=None,
 991 |         help='Explicit path to libclang.so',
 992 |     )
 993 |     args = parser.parse_args()
 994 | 
 995 |     print("Clang commit processor starting", file=sys.stderr)
 996 |     print(f"  inputs: {args.inputs}", file=sys.stderr)
 997 |     print(f"  output: {args.output}", file=sys.stderr)
 998 |     print(f"  max_tokens: {args.max_tokens}", file=sys.stderr)
 999 |     print(f"  format: {args.doc_format}", file=sys.stderr)
1000 | 
1001 |     # Configure libclang
1002 |     libclang_path = _configure_libclang(args.libclang_path)
1003 |     print(f"  libclang: {libclang_path or 'system default'}", file=sys.stderr)
1004 | 
1005 |     # Load tokenizer
1006 |     _load_tokenizer(args.tokenizer_path)
1007 |     if _tokenizer:
1008 |         print(f"  tokenizer: {args.tokenizer_path}", file=sys.stderr)
1009 |     else:
1010 |         print("  tokenizer: estimate (~4 bytes/token)", file=sys.stderr)
1011 | 
1012 |     clang_index = Index.create()
1013 | 
1014 |     t0 = time.time()
1015 |     total_stats = {
1016 |         'records_read': 0,
1017 |         'documents_written': 0,
1018 |         'records_skipped': 0,
1019 |         'records_empty': 0,
1020 |         'parse_errors': 0,
1021 |     }
1022 | 
1023 |     with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory(prefix='clang_commits_') as tmpdir:
1024 |         # Use /dev/shm if available for faster temp file I/O
1025 |         shm_tmpdir = None
1026 |         if os.path.isdir('/dev/shm'):
1027 |             shm_tmpdir = tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix='clang_commits_', dir='/dev/shm')
1028 |             actual_tmpdir = shm_tmpdir
1029 |         else:
1030 |             actual_tmpdir = tmpdir
1031 | 
1032 |         try:
1033 |             with open(args.output, 'w') as out_f:
1034 |                 for input_path in args.inputs:
1035 |                     if not os.path.exists(input_path):
1036 |                         print(f"  WARN: {input_path} not found, skipping", file=sys.stderr)
1037 |                         continue
1038 | 
1039 |                     print(f"\n  Processing {input_path}...", file=sys.stderr)
1040 |                     stats = process_jsonl_file(
1041 |                         input_path, out_f, clang_index, actual_tmpdir,
1042 |                         args.max_tokens, args.max_file_bytes,
1043 |                         args.doc_format, args.max_dep_depth,
1044 |                     )
1045 | 
1046 |                     for k in total_stats:
1047 |                         total_stats[k] += stats[k]
1048 | 
1049 |                     print(f"  Done: {stats}", file=sys.stderr)
1050 |         finally:
1051 |             if shm_tmpdir:
1052 |                 import shutil
1053 |                 shutil.rmtree(shm_tmpdir, ignore_errors=True)
1054 | 
1055 |     elapsed = time.time() - t0
1056 |     print(f"\nTotal: {total_stats}", file=sys.stderr)
1057 |     print(f"Time: {elapsed:.1f}s", file=sys.stderr)
1058 |     if total_stats['records_read'] > 0:
1059 |         rate = total_stats['records_read'] / elapsed
1060 |         print(f"Rate: {rate:.1f} records/sec", file=sys.stderr)
1061 | 
1062 | 
1063 | if __name__ == '__main__':
1064 |     main()
```

## File: /Volumes/external/sources/nanochat/scripts/data/extract_all_commits.py

### Code

```python
  1 | """Extract raw git commit data from ALL repos in parallel.
  2 | 
  3 | Runs extract_git_history.py as subprocesses across N repos simultaneously.
  4 | Each repo gets its own output JSONL file. After all repos finish, concatenates
  5 | into a single file.
  6 | 
  7 | Usage:
  8 |     python3 scripts/data/extract_all_commits.py \
  9 |         --repo_dir ~/data/cpp_raw \
 10 |         --output_dir ~/data/raw_commits_all \
 11 |         --workers 40
 12 | """
 13 | 
 14 | import argparse
 15 | import json
 16 | import os
 17 | import subprocess
 18 | import sys
 19 | import time
 20 | from concurrent.futures import ProcessPoolExecutor, as_completed
 21 | from pathlib import Path
 22 | 
 23 | 
 24 | def extract_repo(args_tuple):
 25 |     """Extract commits from a single repo (runs as subprocess)."""
 26 |     repo_path, output_file, script_path = args_tuple
 27 |     repo_name = Path(repo_path).name
 28 | 
 29 |     # Check if output already exists and has content
 30 |     if os.path.exists(output_file):
 31 |         size = os.path.getsize(output_file)
 32 |         if size > 0:
 33 |             # Count lines to verify it's not corrupt
 34 |             try:
 35 |                 with open(output_file) as f:
 36 |                     lines = sum(1 for _ in f)
 37 |                 if lines > 0:
 38 |                     return {
 39 |                         "repo": repo_name,
 40 |                         "records": lines,
 41 |                         "status": "skipped (already exists)",
 42 |                         "time": 0,
 43 |                     }
 44 |             except Exception:
 45 |                 pass  # Re-extract if corrupt
 46 | 
 47 |     start = time.time()
 48 |     try:
 49 |         result = subprocess.run(
 50 |             [
 51 |                 sys.executable,
 52 |                 script_path,
 53 |                 "--repo",
 54 |                 repo_path,
 55 |                 "--output",
 56 |                 output_file,
 57 |             ],
 58 |             capture_output=True,
 59 |             text=True,
 60 |             timeout=7200,  # 2 hour timeout per repo
 61 |         )
 62 | 
 63 |         elapsed = time.time() - start
 64 | 
 65 |         # Count output records
 66 |         records = 0
 67 |         if os.path.exists(output_file):
 68 |             with open(output_file) as f:
 69 |                 records = sum(1 for _ in f)
 70 | 
 71 |         if result.returncode != 0:
 72 |             return {
 73 |                 "repo": repo_name,
 74 |                 "records": records,
 75 |                 "status": f"error (rc={result.returncode})",
 76 |                 "time": elapsed,
 77 |                 "stderr": result.stderr[-500:] if result.stderr else "",
 78 |             }
 79 | 
 80 |         return {
 81 |             "repo": repo_name,
 82 |             "records": records,
 83 |             "status": "ok",
 84 |             "time": elapsed,
 85 |         }
 86 | 
 87 |     except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
 88 |         elapsed = time.time() - start
 89 |         records = 0
 90 |         if os.path.exists(output_file):
 91 |             with open(output_file) as f:
 92 |                 records = sum(1 for _ in f)
 93 |         return {
 94 |             "repo": repo_name,
 95 |             "records": records,
 96 |             "status": "timeout (2h)",
 97 |             "time": elapsed,
 98 |         }
 99 |     except Exception as e:
100 |         return {
101 |             "repo": repo_name,
102 |             "records": 0,
103 |             "status": f"exception: {e}",
104 |             "time": time.time() - start,
105 |         }
106 | 
107 | 
108 | def main():
109 |     parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(
110 |         description="Extract raw git commits from all repos in parallel"
111 |     )
112 |     parser.add_argument(
113 |         "--repo_dir", required=True, help="Directory containing git repos"
114 |     )
115 |     parser.add_argument(
116 |         "--output_dir", required=True, help="Directory for per-repo JSONL outputs"
117 |     )
118 |     parser.add_argument(
119 |         "--workers", type=int, default=40, help="Parallel extraction workers"
120 |     )
121 |     parser.add_argument(
122 |         "--concat_output",
123 |         default="",
124 |         help="If set, concatenate all per-repo files into this single JSONL",
125 |     )
126 |     parser.add_argument(
127 |         "--only",
128 |         nargs="*",
129 |         help="Only process these repos (by name)",
130 |     )
131 |     parser.add_argument(
132 |         "--exclude",
133 |         nargs="*",
134 |         default=[],
135 |         help="Skip these repos (by name)",
136 |     )
137 |     args = parser.parse_args()
138 | 
139 |     script_path = os.path.join(
140 |         os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)), "extract_git_history.py"
141 |     )
142 |     if not os.path.exists(script_path):
143 |         print(f"ERROR: {script_path} not found")
144 |         sys.exit(1)
145 | 
146 |     os.makedirs(args.output_dir, exist_ok=True)
147 | 
148 |     # Find all repos with .git directory (skip .bare repos)
149 |     repos = []
150 |     exclude_set = set(args.exclude or [])
151 |     only_set = set(args.only) if args.only else None
152 | 
153 |     for entry in sorted(os.listdir(args.repo_dir)):
154 |         if entry.endswith(".bare"):
155 |             continue
156 |         path = os.path.join(args.repo_dir, entry)
157 |         if not os.path.isdir(path):
158 |             continue
159 |         git_dir = os.path.join(path, ".git")
160 |         if not os.path.exists(git_dir):
161 |             continue
162 |         if entry in exclude_set:
163 |             continue
164 |         if only_set and entry not in only_set:
165 |             continue
166 |         repos.append(path)
167 | 
168 |     print(f"Found {len(repos)} repositories in {args.repo_dir}")
169 |     print(f"Output directory: {args.output_dir}")
170 |     print(f"Workers: {args.workers}")
171 |     print()
172 | 
173 |     # Build task list
174 |     tasks = []
175 |     for repo_path in repos:
176 |         repo_name = Path(repo_path).name
177 |         output_file = os.path.join(args.output_dir, f"{repo_name}_commits.jsonl")
178 |         tasks.append((repo_path, output_file, script_path))
179 | 
180 |     # Run in parallel
181 |     total_records = 0
182 |     completed = 0
183 |     errors = 0
184 |     skipped = 0
185 |     start_time = time.time()
186 | 
187 |     results = []
188 |     with ProcessPoolExecutor(max_workers=args.workers) as executor:
189 |         futures = {executor.submit(extract_repo, task): task for task in tasks}
190 | 
191 |         for future in as_completed(futures):
192 |             result = future.result()
193 |             results.append(result)
194 |             completed += 1
195 |             total_records += result["records"]
196 | 
197 |             status_icon = "OK" if result["status"] == "ok" else (
198 |                 "SKIP" if "skipped" in result["status"] else "ERR"
199 |             )
200 |             if "skipped" in result["status"]:
201 |                 skipped += 1
202 |             elif result["status"] != "ok":
203 |                 errors += 1
204 | 
205 |             elapsed_total = time.time() - start_time
206 |             print(
207 |                 f"[{completed}/{len(tasks)}] {status_icon} {result['repo']}: "
208 |                 f"{result['records']:,} records in {result['time']:.0f}s "
209 |                 f"({result['status']}) | total: {total_records:,} records, "
210 |                 f"{elapsed_total:.0f}s elapsed"
211 |             )
212 | 
213 |     # Sort results by record count (descending) for the summary
214 |     results.sort(key=lambda r: r["records"], reverse=True)
215 | 
216 |     elapsed_total = time.time() - start_time
217 |     print("\n" + "=" * 70)
218 |     print("EXTRACTION COMPLETE")
219 |     print("=" * 70)
220 |     print(f"Repos processed: {len(tasks)}")
221 |     print(f"  Successful: {completed - errors - skipped}")
222 |     print(f"  Skipped (existing): {skipped}")
223 |     print(f"  Errors: {errors}")
224 |     print(f"Total records: {total_records:,}")
225 |     print(f"Total time: {elapsed_total:.0f}s ({elapsed_total / 60:.1f}m)")
226 | 
227 |     # Top 20 repos by records
228 |     print("\nTop 20 repos by record count:")
229 |     for r in results[:20]:
230 |         print(f"  {r['repo']:40s} {r['records']:>10,}")
231 | 
232 |     # Save stats
233 |     stats_file = os.path.join(args.output_dir, "extraction_stats.json")
234 |     with open(stats_file, "w") as f:
235 |         json.dump(
236 |             {
237 |                 "total_repos": len(tasks),
238 |                 "total_records": total_records,
239 |                 "errors": errors,
240 |                 "skipped": skipped,
241 |                 "elapsed_seconds": elapsed_total,
242 |                 "per_repo": results,
243 |             },
244 |             f,
245 |             indent=2,
246 |         )
247 |     print(f"\nStats saved to: {stats_file}")
248 | 
249 |     # Concatenate if requested
250 |     if args.concat_output:
251 |         print(f"\nConcatenating to {args.concat_output}...")
252 |         total_lines = 0
253 |         with open(args.concat_output, "w") as out:
254 |             for repo_path in repos:
255 |                 repo_name = Path(repo_path).name
256 |                 per_repo_file = os.path.join(
257 |                     args.output_dir, f"{repo_name}_commits.jsonl"
258 |                 )
259 |                 if not os.path.exists(per_repo_file):
260 |                     continue
261 |                 with open(per_repo_file) as inp:
262 |                     for line in inp:
263 |                         out.write(line)
264 |                         total_lines += 1
265 |         size_gb = os.path.getsize(args.concat_output) / (1024**3)
266 |         print(f"Concatenated: {total_lines:,} records, {size_gb:.2f} GB")
267 | 
268 | 
269 | if __name__ == "__main__":
270 |     main()
```

## File: /Volumes/external/sources/nanochat/scripts/data/extract_git_history.py

### Code

```python
  1 | """Extract git commit history as raw JSONL for the Rust cpp-chunker commit mode.
  2 | 
  3 | Extracts per-file commit diffs from git repos and outputs JSONL with:
  4 |   {old_content, new_content, diff, subject, body, filepath, repo}
  5 | 
  6 | The Rust cpp-chunker --commit-mode then processes these with tree-sitter to
  7 | extract function/class chains and produce training documents.
  8 | 
  9 | Usage:
 10 |     # Extract raw commit data
 11 |     python3 scripts/data/extract_git_history.py \
 12 |         --repo ~/data/cpp_raw/opencv \
 13 |         --output /mnt/nvme/nanochat_data/opencv_commits.jsonl \
 14 |         --max_commits 50000
 15 | 
 16 |     # Process with Rust tool
 17 |     ./cpp-chunker --commit-mode --inputs opencv_commits.jsonl \
 18 |         --output opencv_training.jsonl --max-tokens 16384
 19 | """
 20 | 
 21 | import argparse
 22 | import hashlib
 23 | import json
 24 | import os
 25 | import subprocess
 26 | import time
 27 | from pathlib import Path
 28 | from typing import Optional, TypedDict
 29 | 
 30 | class _ExtractionStats(TypedDict):
 31 |     repo: str
 32 |     commits_checked: int
 33 |     records_written: int
 34 | 
 35 | 
 36 | # C/C++ file extensions
 37 | CPP_EXTENSIONS = {
 38 |     ".c",
 39 |     ".cc",
 40 |     ".cpp",
 41 |     ".cxx",
 42 |     ".c++",
 43 |     ".h",
 44 |     ".hh",
 45 |     ".hpp",
 46 |     ".hxx",
 47 |     ".h++",
 48 |     ".inl",
 49 |     ".inc",
 50 |     ".ipp",
 51 |     ".tcc",
 52 |     ".tpp",
 53 | }
 54 | 
 55 | # Files/paths to skip
 56 | SKIP_PATTERNS = {
 57 |     "test/",
 58 |     "tests/",
 59 |     "testing/",
 60 |     "unittest/",
 61 |     "benchmarks/",
 62 |     "third_party/",
 63 |     "3rdparty/",
 64 |     "vendor/",
 65 |     "external/",
 66 |     "deps/",
 67 |     "generated/",
 68 |     "auto_generated/",
 69 |     "cmake-build",
 70 |     ".pb.h",
 71 |     ".pb.cc",
 72 |     "_generated.h",
 73 |     ".gen.cc",
 74 |     ".gen.h",
 75 | }
 76 | 
 77 | MAX_DIFF_CHARS = 50000
 78 | MAX_FILES_PER_COMMIT = 5
 79 | MIN_DIFF_CHARS = 50
 80 | 
 81 | 
 82 | def stable_repo_id(repo_name: str) -> str:
 83 |     return hashlib.sha1(repo_name.encode("utf-8")).hexdigest()[:16]
 84 | 
 85 | 
 86 | def stable_filepath_id(repo_name: str, filepath: str) -> str:
 87 |     key = f"{repo_name}\0{filepath}"
 88 |     return hashlib.sha1(key.encode("utf-8")).hexdigest()[:16]
 89 | 
 90 | 
 91 | def is_cpp_file(path: str) -> bool:
 92 |     return Path(path).suffix.lower() in CPP_EXTENSIONS
 93 | 
 94 | 
 95 | def should_skip_path(path: str) -> bool:
 96 |     path_lower = path.lower()
 97 |     return any(p in path_lower for p in SKIP_PATTERNS)
 98 | 
 99 | 
100 | def run_git(repo_path: str, args: list[str], timeout: int = 60) -> Optional[str]:
101 |     cmd = ["git", "-C", repo_path] + args
102 |     try:
103 |         result = subprocess.run(
104 |             cmd, capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=timeout, errors="replace"
105 |         )
106 |         if result.returncode != 0:
107 |             return None
108 |         return result.stdout
109 |     except (subprocess.TimeoutExpired, OSError):
110 |         return None
111 | 
112 | 
113 | def get_commit_list(repo_path: str, max_commits: int = 0) -> list[str]:
114 |     args = ["log", "--format=%H", "--no-merges", "--diff-filter=M"]
115 |     if max_commits > 0:
116 |         args.extend(["-n", str(max_commits)])
117 |     output = run_git(repo_path, args, timeout=120)
118 |     if not output:
119 |         return []
120 |     return output.strip().split("\n")
121 | 
122 | 
123 | def get_commit_info(repo_path: str, commit_hash: str) -> Optional[dict]:
124 |     fmt = "%H%n%s%n%b%n%P%n%aI%n%cI"
125 |     output = run_git(repo_path, ["show", "-s", f"--format={fmt}", commit_hash])
126 |     if not output:
127 |         return None
128 |     lines = output.splitlines()
129 |     if len(lines) < 5:
130 |         return None
131 |     subject = lines[1]
132 |     body_lines = lines[2:-3]
133 |     parent_hashes = [item for item in lines[-3].strip().split() if item]
134 |     author_timestamp = lines[-2].strip() or None
135 |     commit_timestamp = lines[-1].strip() or None
136 |     return {
137 |         "hash": lines[0],
138 |         "subject": subject,
139 |         "body": "\n".join(body_lines).strip(),
140 |         "parent_hashes": parent_hashes,
141 |         "parent_count": len(parent_hashes),
142 |         "is_merge_commit": len(parent_hashes) > 1,
143 |         "author_timestamp": author_timestamp,
144 |         "commit_timestamp": commit_timestamp,
145 |         "timestamp": commit_timestamp or author_timestamp,
146 |     }
147 | 
148 | 
149 | def compute_file_local_commit_indices(
150 |     repo_path: str,
151 |     commit_hashes: list[str],
152 | ) -> dict[tuple[str, str], int]:
153 |     counters: dict[str, int] = {}
154 |     indices: dict[tuple[str, str], int] = {}
155 |     for commit_hash in reversed(commit_hashes):
156 |         file_diffs = get_commit_diffs(repo_path, commit_hash)
157 |         if not file_diffs:
158 |             continue
159 |         for item in file_diffs:
160 |             filepath = item["filepath"]
161 |             next_index = counters.get(filepath, 0)
162 |             indices[(commit_hash, filepath)] = next_index
163 |             counters[filepath] = next_index + 1
164 |     return indices
165 | 
166 | 
167 | def get_commit_diffs(repo_path: str, commit_hash: str) -> Optional[list[dict]]:
168 |     """Get per-file diffs for C/C++ files in a commit."""
169 |     name_status = run_git(
170 |         repo_path, ["diff-tree", "--no-commit-id", "-r", "--name-status", commit_hash]
171 |     )
172 |     if not name_status:
173 |         return None
174 | 
175 |     files = []
176 |     for line in name_status.strip().split("\n"):
177 |         if not line.strip():
178 |             continue
179 |         parts = line.split("\t")
180 |         if len(parts) < 2:
181 |             continue
182 |         status = parts[0][0]
183 |         filepath = parts[-1]
184 |         if not is_cpp_file(filepath):
185 |             continue
186 |         if should_skip_path(filepath):
187 |             continue
188 |         if status not in ("M",):
189 |             continue
190 |         files.append(filepath)
191 | 
192 |     if not files or len(files) > MAX_FILES_PER_COMMIT:
193 |         return None
194 | 
195 |     results = []
196 |     for filepath in files:
197 |         old_content = run_git(
198 |             repo_path, ["show", f"{commit_hash}^:{filepath}"], timeout=30
199 |         )
200 |         new_content = run_git(
201 |             repo_path, ["show", f"{commit_hash}:{filepath}"], timeout=30
202 |         )
203 |         diff = run_git(
204 |             repo_path,
205 |             ["diff", f"{commit_hash}^", commit_hash, "--", filepath],
206 |             timeout=30,
207 |         )
208 | 
209 |         if old_content is None or new_content is None or diff is None:
210 |             continue
211 |         diff_len = len(diff)
212 |         if diff_len < MIN_DIFF_CHARS or diff_len > MAX_DIFF_CHARS:
213 |             continue
214 |         if len(old_content) > 200000 or len(new_content) > 200000:
215 |             continue
216 | 
217 |         results.append(
218 |             {
219 |                 "filepath": filepath,
220 |                 "old_content": old_content,
221 |                 "new_content": new_content,
222 |                 "diff": diff,
223 |             }
224 |         )
225 | 
226 |     return results if results else None
227 | 
228 | 
229 | def process_repo(
230 |     repo_path: str,
231 |     output_file,
232 |     max_commits: int = 0,
233 |     repo_name: str = "",
234 | ) -> _ExtractionStats:
235 |     if not repo_name:
236 |         repo_name = Path(repo_path).name
237 | 
238 |     stats: _ExtractionStats = {
239 |         "repo": repo_name,
240 |         "commits_checked": 0,
241 |         "records_written": 0,
242 |     }
243 | 
244 |     depth_output = run_git(repo_path, ["rev-list", "--count", "HEAD"])
245 |     if depth_output:
246 |         commit_count = int(depth_output.strip())
247 |         if commit_count <= 1:
248 |             print(f"  [{repo_name}] Shallow clone (1 commit), skipping")
249 |             return stats
250 |         print(f"  [{repo_name}] {commit_count:,} commits available")
251 |     else:
252 |         print(f"  [{repo_name}] Cannot count commits, skipping")
253 |         return stats
254 | 
255 |     commits = get_commit_list(repo_path, max_commits)
256 |     if not commits:
257 |         print(f"  [{repo_name}] No commits found")
258 |         return stats
259 | 
260 |     file_local_commit_indices = compute_file_local_commit_indices(repo_path, commits)
261 | 
262 |     for i, commit_hash in enumerate(commits):
263 |         if i > 0 and i % 1000 == 0:
264 |             print(
265 |                 f"  [{repo_name}] Processed {i:,}/{len(commits):,} commits, "
266 |                 f"{stats['records_written']:,} records"
267 |             )
268 | 
269 |         stats["commits_checked"] += 1
270 | 
271 |         commit_info = get_commit_info(repo_path, commit_hash)
272 |         if not commit_info:
273 |             continue
274 | 
275 |         subject = commit_info["subject"].lower()
276 |         if any(
277 |             skip in subject
278 |             for skip in [
279 |                 "merge branch",
280 |                 "merge pull request",
281 |                 "update submodule",
282 |                 "bump version",
283 |                 "auto-generated",
284 |                 "clang-format",
285 |                 "fix whitespace",
286 |                 "fix typo in comment",
287 |             ]
288 |         ):
289 |             continue
290 | 
291 |         file_diffs = get_commit_diffs(repo_path, commit_hash)
292 |         if not file_diffs:
293 |             continue
294 | 
295 |         for fd in file_diffs:
296 |             record = {
297 |                 "old_content": fd["old_content"],
298 |                 "new_content": fd["new_content"],
299 |                 "diff": fd["diff"],
300 |                 "subject": commit_info["subject"],
301 |                 "body": commit_info["body"],
302 |                 "filepath": fd["filepath"],
303 |                 "repo": repo_name,
304 |                 "commit_hash": commit_info["hash"],
305 |                 "timestamp": commit_info["timestamp"],
306 |                 "parent_hashes": list(commit_info["parent_hashes"]),
307 |                 "parent_count": int(commit_info["parent_count"]),
308 |                 "is_merge_commit": bool(commit_info["is_merge_commit"]),
309 |                 "author_timestamp": commit_info["author_timestamp"],
310 |                 "commit_timestamp": commit_info["commit_timestamp"],
311 |                 "repo_stable_id": stable_repo_id(repo_name),
312 |                 "filepath_stable_id": stable_filepath_id(repo_name, fd["filepath"]),
313 |                 "file_local_commit_index": int(
314 |                     file_local_commit_indices[(commit_hash, fd["filepath"])]
315 |                 ),
316 |                 "has_ambiguous_reconstruction": False,
317 |                 "has_rename_ambiguity": False,
318 |             }
319 |             output_file.write(json.dumps(record) + "\n")
320 |             stats["records_written"] += 1
321 | 
322 |     return stats
323 | 
324 | 
325 | def main():
326 |     parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(
327 |         description="Extract raw git commit data as JSONL for cpp-chunker --commit-mode"
328 |     )
329 |     group = parser.add_mutually_exclusive_group(required=True)
330 |     group.add_argument("--repo", help="Path to a single git repository")
331 |     group.add_argument("--repo_dir", help="Directory containing multiple repos")
332 |     parser.add_argument("--output", required=True, help="Output JSONL file")
333 |     parser.add_argument(
334 |         "--max_commits", type=int, default=0, help="Max commits per repo (0 = all)"
335 |     )
336 |     args = parser.parse_args()
337 | 
338 |     repos = []
339 |     if args.repo:
340 |         repos.append(args.repo)
341 |     else:
342 |         for entry in sorted(os.listdir(args.repo_dir)):
343 |             path = os.path.join(args.repo_dir, entry)
344 |             if os.path.isdir(os.path.join(path, ".git")):
345 |                 repos.append(path)
346 | 
347 |     print(f"Found {len(repos)} repositories")
348 |     print(f"Max commits per repo: {args.max_commits or 'all'}")
349 |     print(f"Output: {args.output}")
350 |     print()
351 | 
352 |     os.makedirs(os.path.dirname(args.output) or ".", exist_ok=True)
353 | 
354 |     total_records = 0
355 |     start_time = time.time()
356 | 
357 |     with open(args.output, "w") as f:
358 |         for i, repo_path in enumerate(repos):
359 |             repo_name = Path(repo_path).name
360 |             print(f"[{i + 1}/{len(repos)}] {repo_name}...")
361 | 
362 |             try:
363 |                 stats = process_repo(repo_path, f, args.max_commits, repo_name)
364 |                 total_records += stats["records_written"]
365 |                 print(f"  [{repo_name}] {stats['records_written']:,} records")
366 |             except Exception as e:
367 |                 print(f"  [{repo_name}] ERROR: {e}")
368 | 
369 |     elapsed = time.time() - start_time
370 |     output_size = os.path.getsize(args.output)
371 |     print("\n=== SUMMARY ===")
372 |     print(f"Repos: {len(repos)}")
373 |     print(f"Total records: {total_records:,}")
374 |     print(f"Time: {elapsed:.0f}s ({elapsed / 60:.1f}m)")
375 |     print(f"Output: {args.output} ({output_size / (1024**3):.2f} GB)")
376 | 
377 | 
378 | if __name__ == "__main__":
379 |     main()
```

```bash
  2 | # Master pipeline: Extract enriched commit data from all repos, multiple token sizes.
  3 | #
  4 | # Phase 1: Extract raw commits from all 332 repos (40 parallel processes)
  5 | # Phase 2: Run cpp-chunker --commit-mode --enriched at 5 token sizes
  6 | # Phase 3: Convert enriched JSONL to parquet shards
  7 | # Phase 4: Upload to GCS
  8 | #
  9 | # Usage:
 10 | #   bash scripts/data/build_enriched_commits.sh [--skip-extract] [--skip-chunk] [--skip-parquet]
 11 | #
 12 | # Prerequisites:
 13 | #   - ~/data/cpp_raw/ contains 332+ git repos with .git dirs
 14 | #   - ~/nanochat/tools/cpp_chunker/target/release/cpp-chunker is built

127 |     for i in "${!TOKEN_SIZES[@]}"; do
128 |         label="${TOKEN_LABELS[$i]}"

150 |     log "Phase 3 complete"
151 | else

153 | fi
154 |

156 | # PHASE 4: Upload to GCS
157 | # ============================================================
158 | log "PHASE 4: Uploading to GCS"

162 |     gcs_dest="${GCS_BUCKET}/enriched_commits_${label}"
163 | 
164 |     if [[ ! -d "$local_parquet" ]]; then

170 |         log "  ${label}: parquet not complete, skipping upload"
171 |         continue
172 |     fi

175 |     gcloud storage cp -r "${local_parquet}/*" "${gcs_dest}/" 2>&1 | tail -3
176 |     log "  ${label}: uploaded"
177 | done
178 |

180 | 
181 | # ============================================================
182 | # SUMMARY
183 | # ============================================================
184 | echo ""
185 | echo "============================================================"
186 | echo "ENRICHED COMMIT PIPELINE COMPLETE"
187 | echo "============================================================"
188 | for i in "${!TOKEN_SIZES[@]}"; do
```

## File: /Volumes/external/sources/nanochat/scripts/data/convert_commitpack.py

- Extension: .py
- Language: python
- Size: 5095 bytes
- Created: 2026-04-02 22:45:34
- Modified: 2026-04-02 22:45:34

### Code

```python
  1 | """Convert CommitPack C++ JSONL files to raw commit records for cpp-chunker --commit-mode.
  2 | 
  3 | Reads CommitPack's (old_contents, new_contents, subject, message) format
  4 | and produces JSONL with {old_content, new_content, diff, subject, body, filepath, repo}
  5 | that the Rust cpp-chunker --commit-mode can process with tree-sitter.
  6 | 
  7 | Usage:
  8 |     python3 scripts/data/convert_commitpack.py \
  9 |         --input_dir ~/data/commitpack_cpp/data/c++ \
 10 |         --output /mnt/nvme/nanochat_data/commitpack_raw_commits.jsonl
 11 | """
 12 | 
 13 | import argparse
 14 | import difflib
 15 | import glob
 16 | import json
 17 | import os
 18 | from pathlib import Path
 19 | 
 20 | # C/C++ file extensions
 21 | CPP_EXTENSIONS = {
 22 |     ".c",
 23 |     ".cc",
 24 |     ".cpp",
 25 |     ".cxx",
 26 |     ".c++",
 27 |     ".h",
 28 |     ".hh",
 29 |     ".hpp",
 30 |     ".hxx",
 31 |     ".h++",
 32 |     ".inl",
 33 |     ".inc",
 34 |     ".ipp",
 35 |     ".tcc",
 36 |     ".tpp",
 37 | }
 38 | 
 39 | 
 40 | def is_cpp_file(path: str) -> bool:
 41 |     return Path(path).suffix.lower() in CPP_EXTENSIONS
 42 | 
 43 | 
 44 | def generate_unified_diff(old_content: str, new_content: str, filepath: str) -> str:
 45 |     """Generate a unified diff from old and new content."""
 46 |     old_lines = old_content.splitlines(keepends=True)
 47 |     new_lines = new_content.splitlines(keepends=True)
 48 |     diff_lines = difflib.unified_diff(
 49 |         old_lines,
 50 |         new_lines,
 51 |         fromfile=f"a/{filepath}",
 52 |         tofile=f"b/{filepath}",
 53 |     )
 54 |     return "".join(diff_lines)
 55 | 
 56 | 
 57 | def main():
 58 |     parser = argparse.ArgumentParser()
 59 |     parser.add_argument("--input_dir", required=True, help="CommitPack C++ JSONL dir")
 60 |     parser.add_argument("--output", required=True, help="Output JSONL file")
 61 |     args = parser.parse_args()
 62 | 
 63 |     jsonl_files = sorted(
 64 |         glob.glob(os.path.join(args.input_dir, "**/*.jsonl"), recursive=True)
 65 |     )
 66 |     if not jsonl_files:
 67 |         jsonl_files = sorted(glob.glob(os.path.join(args.input_dir, "*.jsonl")))
 68 |     print(f"Found {len(jsonl_files)} JSONL files")
 69 | 
 70 |     os.makedirs(os.path.dirname(args.output) or ".", exist_ok=True)
 71 | 
 72 |     total = 0
 73 |     skipped = 0
 74 |     errors = 0
 75 | 
 76 |     with open(args.output, "w") as out:
 77 |         for jf in jsonl_files:
 78 |             file_docs = 0
 79 |             with open(jf) as inp:
 80 |                 for line in inp:
 81 |                     try:
 82 |                         row = json.loads(line)
 83 |                     except json.JSONDecodeError:
 84 |                         errors += 1
 85 |                         continue
 86 | 
 87 |                     subject = str(row.get("subject", ""))
 88 |                     message = str(row.get("message", ""))
 89 |                     old_contents = str(row.get("old_contents", ""))
 90 |                     new_contents = str(row.get("new_contents", ""))
 91 |                     old_file = str(row.get("old_file", ""))
 92 |                     new_file = str(row.get("new_file", ""))
 93 |                     repos = str(row.get("repos", ""))
 94 | 
 95 |                     filepath = new_file or old_file
 96 |                     if not is_cpp_file(filepath):
 97 |                         skipped += 1
 98 |                         continue
 99 | 
100 |                     if len(old_contents) < 50 or len(new_contents) < 50:
101 |                         skipped += 1
102 |                         continue
103 |                     if len(old_contents) > 200000 or len(new_contents) > 200000:
104 |                         skipped += 1
105 |                         continue
106 | 
107 |                     subj_lower = subject.lower()
108 |                     if any(
109 |                         s in subj_lower
110 |                         for s in [
111 |                             "merge branch",
112 |                             "merge pull",
113 |                             "bump version",
114 |                             "auto-generated",
115 |                             "clang-format",
116 |                             "fix whitespace",
117 |                         ]
118 |                     ):
119 |                         skipped += 1
120 |                         continue
121 | 
122 |                     # Generate unified diff
123 |                     diff = generate_unified_diff(old_contents, new_contents, filepath)
124 |                     if len(diff) < 50 or len(diff) > 50000:
125 |                         skipped += 1
126 |                         continue
127 | 
128 |                     repo_name = repos.split(",")[0].strip() if repos else "unknown"
129 |                     body = message if message != subject else ""
130 | 
131 |                     record = {
132 |                         "old_content": old_contents,
133 |                         "new_content": new_contents,
134 |                         "diff": diff,
135 |                         "subject": subject,
136 |                         "body": body,
137 |                         "filepath": filepath,
138 |                         "repo": repo_name,
139 |                     }
140 |                     out.write(json.dumps(record) + "\n")
141 |                     total += 1
142 |                     file_docs += 1
143 | 
144 |                     if total % 100000 == 0 and total > 0:
145 |                         print(f"    {total:,} records so far...")
146 | 
147 |             print(f"  {os.path.basename(jf)}: {file_docs:,} records")
148 | 
149 |     size_gb = os.path.getsize(args.output) / (1024**3)
150 |     print(f"\nTotal: {total:,} records ({skipped:,} skipped, {errors} errors)")
151 |     print(f"Output: {args.output} ({size_gb:.2f} GB)")
152 | 
153 | 
154 | if __name__ == "__main__":
155 |     main()
```

```bash
 3 | # CommitPack: 4TB of git commits across 350 languages (OctoPack/BigCode)

 5 | #
```

## File: /Volumes/external/sources/nanochat/scripts/data/translate_commits.py

- Extension: .py
- Language: python
- Size: 35915 bytes
- Created: 2026-04-07 16:24:46
- Modified: 2026-04-07 16:24:46

### Code

```python
  1 | #!/usr/bin/env python3
  2 | """Translate non-English comments in CommitPack JSONL records.
  3 | 
  4 | Streams a raw commit JSONL file, extracts C++ comments containing non-ASCII
  5 | characters, applies symbol replacements where possible, sends the rest to
  6 | Gemini for translation, and writes clean output with all comments in English.
  7 | 
  8 | Records that cannot be fully translated are DROPPED from output.
  9 | 
 10 | Usage:
 11 |     python translate_commits.py \
 12 |         --input commits_raw.jsonl \
 13 |         --output commits_translated.jsonl \
 14 |         --workers 8
 15 | 
 16 | Requires: pip install google-genai pydantic
 17 | Env: GEMINI_API_KEY must be set
 18 | """
```
